Now, we are ready to present the proofs of the theorems in Section
2
.

S.1.1
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Let

\tilde{T}_{ij}\doteq\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}=\frac{z_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{j}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}},\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\rho_{ij}=\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{\|\epsilon_{i\cdot}\|\|\epsilon_{j\cdot}\|}.

To simplify notation, we define
z_{i}\doteq(\mathbf{Z})_{i\cdot},
where
(\mathbf{Z})_{i\cdot}
represents the i-th row vector of
\mathbf{Z},
for
1\leq i\leq N.
Hence, we have

\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}=\mathbf{P}_{i}\epsilon_{i\cdot}=\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}z_{i}.

Lemma S.1.5
.

Feng et al. 2022

Set
\mathbf{B}_{i}=\mathbf{x}_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime},
for
1\leq i\leq N.

Then,

\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|\leq 14\cdot\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq j\leq N}\frac{{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}{{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}\right).

We now are ready to derive the asymptotic null distribution of
L_{N}.

First, we will show that for
y\in\mathbb{R}
, under
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\tilde{T}_{ij}^{2}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

(S.1.2)

By Theorem 1 in
Arratia et al. 1989
, we have

\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\tilde{T}_{ij}^{2}\leq t_{N}\right)-e^{-\tau_{N}}\right|\leq b_{1N}+b_{2N}+b_{3N}

where

t_{N}=4\log N-\log\log N+y,\tau_{N}=\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N}\right),

\displaystyle b_{1N}=\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}\sum_{(k,l)\in B_{ij}}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{ij}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{kl}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\leq N^{3}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\right]^{2},

\displaystyle b_{2N}=\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}\sum_{(k,l)\in B_{ij}\backslash\{(i,j)\}}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{ij}^{2}>t_{N},\tilde{T}_{kl}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\leq N^{3}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N},\tilde{T}_{13}^{2}>t_{N}\right),

\displaystyle b_{3N}=\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbb{P}\left\{\tilde{T}_{ij}^{2}>t_{N}|\sigma(\tilde{T}_{kl}^{2}:(k,l)\notin B_{ij})\right\}-\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\right|=0,

for all
1\leq i<j\leq N
,
B_{ij}\doteq\{(k,l):1\leq k<l\leq N,\{k,l\}\cap\{i,j\}\neq\varnothing\},

and the third term
b_{3N}
on the right side of the inequality is equal to zero because for four different indices
i,j,k,l,

\tilde{T}_{ij}
and
\tilde{T}_{kl}
are independent.
Note that according to Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
, we have for any large
M>0
, there exists some
C_{1}>0
such
that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}>1+\varepsilon_{1}\right)\leq 2N^{-M},

where
\varepsilon_{1}=C_{1}\sqrt{\log N/\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}.

By Corollary 3.1 in
Saulis and Statulevicius 1991
and
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})=\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}\right|>\sqrt{t_{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}>\sqrt{t_{N}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}>\sqrt{t_{N}},\frac{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}\leq 1+\varepsilon_{1}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}>1+\varepsilon_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}>\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1+\varepsilon_{1}}\right\}+2N^{-M}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{I\left(\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}>\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1+\varepsilon_{1}}\right)\Big|z_{2}\right\}\right]+2N^{-M}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}>\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1+\varepsilon_{1}}\Big|z_{2}\right)\right\}+2N^{-M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{1+o(1)\}\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi t_{N}}}\exp^{-t_{N}/2}+2N^{-M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(N^{-2}),

(S.1.3)

where last equality holds due to Proposition 2.1.2 in
Vershynin 2018
,

I(\cdot)
denotes indicative function, and
M
is sufficiently large. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}\leq t_{N}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}\right|\leq\sqrt{t_{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}\leq\sqrt{t_{N}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}\leq\sqrt{t_{N}},\frac{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}\geq 1-\varepsilon_{1}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}<1-\varepsilon_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}\right\}+2N^{-M},

then
1-\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}<t_{N}\right)\geq 1-\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}<\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}\right\}-2N^{-M}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{2}|}{\sqrt{z_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}z_{2}}}>\frac{\sqrt{t_{N}}}{1-\varepsilon_{1}}\right\}-2N^{-M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{1-o(1)\}\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi t_{N}}}\exp^{-t_{N}/2}-2N^{-M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(N^{-2}).

This shows that
\tau_{N}\sim\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}e^{-y/2}
and
b_{1N}\leq CN^{-1}
. For
b_{2N}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{T}_{12}^{2}>t_{N},\tilde{T}_{13}^{2}>t_{N}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}-\tilde{T}_{13}\right|\geq 2\sqrt{t_{N}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}+\tilde{T}_{13}\right|\geq 2\sqrt{t_{N}}\right).

Again, by Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
, for any large
M>0
, there exists some
C_{2}>0
such
that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}(z_{2}-z_{3})}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}>2(1+\varepsilon_{2})\right)\leq 2N^{-M},

where
\varepsilon_{2}=C_{2}\sqrt{\log N/\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}.

By Corollary 3.1 (Cramér type moderate deviation results) in
Saulis and Statulevicius 1991
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}-\tilde{T}_{13}\right|\geq 2\sqrt{t_{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\left|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}\left(z_{2}-z_{3}\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}\geq 2\sqrt{t_{N}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\left|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}\left(z_{2}-z_{3}\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}}\geq 2\sqrt{t_{N}},\frac{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}(z_{2}-z_{3})}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}<2(1+\varepsilon_{2})\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}(z_{2}-z_{3})}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})}>2(1+\varepsilon_{2})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\left|z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}\left(z_{2}-z_{3}\right)\right|}{\sqrt{(z_{2}-z_{3})^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}(z_{2}-z_{3})}}\geq\frac{\sqrt{2t_{N}}}{1+\varepsilon_{2}}\right\}+2N^{-M}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\{1+o(1)\}\frac{\sqrt{\log N}}{2\sqrt{\pi}}N^{-4}e^{-y},

where
M
is sufficiently large.
Similarly,
\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\tilde{T}_{12}+\tilde{T}_{13}\right|\geq 2\sqrt{t_{n}}\right)=\{1+o(1)\}\frac{\sqrt{\log N}}{2\sqrt{\pi}}N^{-4}e^{-y}
. Combining these inequalities, we have
b_{2N}\leq CN^{-1}\sqrt{\log N}
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow G(y)=\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

(S.1.4)

Similarly, due to Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}-\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\right|\geq C^{\prime}\sqrt{T\log N}\right)=O\left(N^{-M}\right),

(S.1.5)

where
M>0
is sufficiently large,
C^{\prime}
is a constant that depends on
M.

Due to (
S.1.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

(S.1.6)

Set

\Omega=\left\{\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T^{2}}+\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T^{2}}-\frac{2\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|\leq 2C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}.

Obviously, due to (
S.1.5
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega^{c}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}+\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{2\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\frac{2C_{1}T}{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}+\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{2\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\frac{2C_{1}}{\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\frac{C_{1}}{\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\frac{C_{1}}{\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}=O\left(N^{-M}\right).

We claim that for some constant
C_{3}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)\rightarrow 0

as
N,T
are sufficiently large.
Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}},\Omega\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega^{c}\right),

we just need to prove that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}},\Omega\right)\rightarrow 0.

In fact, due to (
S.1.5
), for sufficiently large
C_{3}
satisfying

C_{3}-2C_{1}>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\sqrt{\frac{C_{3}}{2}}>C_{1},

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}},\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}-\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})-\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)-\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)+2\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{2T}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\left(\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right)\left(\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right)\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{2T}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\sqrt{C_{3}}\left(\frac{\log N}{2T}\right)^{1/4}\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\sqrt{C_{3}}\left(\frac{\log N}{2T}\right)^{1/4}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{C_{3}\log N}{2T}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}{T}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{C_{3}\log N}{2T}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(N^{-M}\right),

where the last inequality holds due to (
S.1.5
).
Then, we can conclude that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)=O\left(N^{-M}\right)

and

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}/T^{2}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})/T^{2}}-1\right|<\frac{C_{3}}{\lambda_{min}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)\rightarrow 1.

Thus, with probability tending to one, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})/T^{2}}{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}/T^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{T^{2}}{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}-\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{T^{2}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}-\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}\right|=O_{p}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right\}.

This, together with (
S.1.6
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\rho^{2}_{ij}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\},

(S.1.7)

for any
y\in\mathbb{R}.

Then, we want to prove that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

So, we just need to show that for any
\epsilon>0

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)\rightarrow 0.

This is because if it holds, we have

\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\log N}\cdot\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}\right|-\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|\right)\rightarrow 0

in probability. Set
\Delta=\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}\right|-\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|+\Delta\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|^{2}+2\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|\Delta+\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\Delta^{2}.

The Slutsky lemma and (
S.1.1
) say that
(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}/\log N)^{1/2}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|\rightarrow 2
in probability. Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|\Delta=\left(\frac{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}{\log N}\right)^{1/2}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|\cdot\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\log N}\Delta\Big)\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\Delta^{2}=\Big[\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\log N}\Delta\Big]^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log N}\rightarrow 0

in probability. Then, we can obtain that

\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}\right|^{2}=\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|{\rho}_{ij}\right|^{2}+o_{p}(1).

So, we will prove

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)\rightarrow 0.

(S.1.8)

By Lemma
S.1.5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}{{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{14\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}}{{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{14\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}}{{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{14\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)},{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}>\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}<\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{28\sqrt{\log N}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{1}<\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{28\sqrt{\log N}}\right)+2\exp\left(-\eta_{0}T\right)\right].

Here, by Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
, there exists a constant
\eta_{0},\,C>0
such
that

\mathbb{P}\left(z_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Sigma}z_{1}<\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{2}\right)\leq C\exp\left(-\eta_{0}T\right).

Note that
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})=\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma})\leq p\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})

and
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\leq\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})\leq p\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})^{2}.

Again, by Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
and Assumption
3
, for some constant
C>0,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{28\sqrt{\log N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\epsilon}_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{1\cdot}>\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})+\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{56\sqrt{\log N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\left\{-C\sqrt{T/\log N}\right\},

where the first inequality holds because that for sufficiently large
N,T
,
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\leq p\lambda_{max}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\leq\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}/\{56\sqrt{\log N}\},
for all
i=1,\cdots,N.

As
p>0
is fixed, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2N^{2}\exp\left(-C\sqrt{T/\log N}\right)+2N^{2}\exp\left(-\eta_{0}T\right)\rightarrow 0.

By the slutsky lemma again, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

(S.1.9)

Then, we complete the proof.
\Box

S.1.2
Proof of Theorem
2.2

Lemma S.1.6
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
2.2
.
For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and sufficiently large
T,

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\left|\hat{\sigma}_{ij}-\sigma_{ij}\right|\geq x\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{ij}^{2}+(1+|\gamma_{2}|)\sigma_{ii}\sigma_{jj}}{N}}\bigg)\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{2}(1-\varepsilon)\right),

uniformly for
x\in(0,N^{\frac{1}{2}\wedge(\frac{1}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2})}),
where
C
does not depend on
i,j.

Lemma S.1.7
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
2.2
. Let
\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}=\big(\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}{T}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq N},\hat{\gamma}_{N}=\|\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\{\mathrm{tr}(\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}})\}^{2}
and
\gamma_{N}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{N}}{N}=\frac{\gamma_{N}}{N}a_{N}+b_{N},

(S.1.10)

where
\left\{a_{N}\right\}
are real numbers satisfying
1-c_{1}/T\leq a_{N}\leq 1+c_{2}N/T
for some constant
c_{1},c_{2}>0,\left\{b_{N}\right\}
are random variables satisfying

\mathbb{E}\left(b_{N}^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{NT}\right).

With preparations earlier, we are now ready to prove Theorem
2.2
.
According to (
S.1.9
), we have

\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})/\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}=O_{p}(\log N).

Notice that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}^{2}_{ij}\Big|\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}\frac{\left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\mathbf{\Sigma}})}-1\Big|.

To obtain the limit null distribution of
L_{N},
we only need to show that

\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}\frac{\left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\mathbf{\Sigma}})}-1=o_{p}\{\log^{-1}N\}.

So,
similar to the proof of Theorem 2.1 in
Chen and Liu 2018
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}\frac{\left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\mathbf{\Sigma}})}=1+O_{P}\bigg\{\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{N}}\Big)^{\min(1,2-\tau)}\bigg\}=1+o_{p}\{\log^{-1}N\},

(S.1.11)

due to Lemmas
S.1.6
and
S.1.7
. Then, we complete the proof and obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}L_{N}-4\log N+\log\log N\leq y\right)\rightarrow\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{y}{2}\right)\right\}.

\Box

S.1.3
Proof of Theorem
2.3

Under the assumptions in Theorem
2.3
, we establish two similar lemmas.

Lemma S.1.8
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
2.3
. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and sufficiently large
T,

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\left|\hat{\sigma}_{ij}-\sigma_{ij}\right|\geq x\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{U}^{2})(\sigma_{ii}\sigma_{jj}+\sigma_{ij}^{2})}{N^{2}}}\bigg)\leq C\exp\left(-C^{\prime}x^{2}(1-\varepsilon)\right),

uniformly for
x\in(0,N^{\frac{1}{2}\wedge(\frac{1}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2})}),
where
C
and
C^{\prime}
do not depend on
i,j.

Lemma S.1.9
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
2.3
. Let
\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}=\big(\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}}{T}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq N},\hat{\gamma}_{N}=\|\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\{\mathrm{tr}(\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}})\}^{2}
and
\gamma_{N}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{N}}{N}=\frac{\gamma_{N}}{N}a_{N}+b_{N},

(S.1.12)

where
\left\{a_{N}\right\}
are real numbers satisfying
1-c_{1}/T\leq a_{N}\leq 1+c_{2}N/T
for some constant
c_{1},c_{2}>0,\left\{b_{N}\right\}
are random variables satisfying

\mathbb{E}\left(b_{N}^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T^{\frac{1}{2}\vee\left(\frac{1}{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}\right)}}\right).

We are now ready to prove Theorem
2.3
.
Because for any
1\leq i\leq j\leq N,

{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}={Z}^{\prime}\left[\{u_{i}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}\right]{Z}

and

{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}_{i}{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}={Z}^{\prime}\left[\{u_{j}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\otimes(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\right]{Z}.

Similar to (
S.1.8
), we can use the similar approach to prove that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\hat{\rho}_{ij}-{\rho}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\epsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\right)}}{\sqrt{\log N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)}\right)\rightarrow 0,

and

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\left|\frac{\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{i\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}}{T^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T^{2}}\right|>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)=O\left(N^{-M}\right).

In addition, similar to the proof of Theorem 2.1 in
Chen and Liu 2018
, we can also prove that

\displaystyle\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}({\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\left\|{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}=1+O_{P}\bigg\{\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{N}}\Big)^{\min(1,2-\tau)}\bigg\},

(S.1.13)

due to Lemmas
S.1.8
and
S.1.9
.
Note that

\sqrt{\mathrm{var}\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)}=\sqrt{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left(u_{ii}u_{jj}+u_{ij}^{2}\right)+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\{u_{i}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\circ\{u_{i}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\right]\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\circ\mathbf{\Sigma})},

where

u_{l}^{1/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
denotes the
l
-th row vector of matrix
\mathbf{U}^{1/2}.
Note that we assume that
(\mathbf{U})_{ii}=1,
for all
1\leq i\leq N,

so, we have

\mathrm{tr}\left[\{u_{i}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\circ\{u_{i}^{1/2}(u_{j}^{1/2})^{\prime}\}\right]\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\circ\mathbf{\Sigma})\leq\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}).

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
u_{12}\geq\delta\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}\log N}
for some constant
\delta>2
.
Note that for some constant
C>0,

by Theorem 1.1 in
Rudelson and Vershynin 2013
and Assumption
3
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\frac{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}-u_{12}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\sqrt{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}>\varepsilon\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-C\varepsilon\log N\right\}\rightarrow 0,

for all
\varepsilon>0.

Then, there exist a constant
M>0
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\hat{\rho}_{ij}>\sqrt{4\log N-\log\log N+w_{\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\hat{\rho}_{12}>\sqrt{4\log N-\log\log N+w_{\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\hat{\rho}_{12}>\sqrt{4\log N-\log\log N+w_{\alpha}}\Big(1+\frac{M\sqrt{\log N}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}{\rho}_{12}>\sqrt{4\log N-\log\log N+w_{\alpha}}\Big(1+\frac{M\sqrt{\log N}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)+o(1)\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}{\rho}_{12}>\sqrt{4\log N-\log\log N+w_{\alpha}}+o(1)\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\frac{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}-\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})u_{12}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{1\cdot}\epsilon_{2\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}}}>-(\delta-2)\sqrt{\log N}/2\right)+o(1),

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}-\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})u_{12}}{\sqrt{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}>-(\delta-2)\sqrt{\log N}/2\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}-\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})u_{12}}{\sqrt{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\leq-(\delta-2)\sqrt{\log N}/2\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{P}\left(\Big|\frac{\epsilon_{1\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{2\cdot}-\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})u_{12}}{\sqrt{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}}\Big|\geq(\delta-2)\sqrt{\log N}/2\right)+o(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-O(N^{-\varepsilon_{1}})+o(1)\rightarrow 1,

where
\varepsilon_{1}=C\{(\delta-2)/2\}^{2}>0,
for some constant
C>0.

Consequently, we complete the proof of this theorem.
\Box

S.1.4
Proof of Theorem
2.4

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{max}=\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot})^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N}=\{(i,j);1\leq i<j\leq N\}

\displaystyle A_{N}=\left\{S_{N}/\sigma_{S_{N}}\leq x\right\}\quad\text{ and }\quad B_{I}=\left\{\left|\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right|\geq l_{N}\right\}

(S.1.14)

for any
I=(i,j)\in\Lambda_{N},
where
a_{N}=4\log N-\log\log N+y

and

l_{N}=\sqrt{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left[4\log N-\log\log N+y\right]}=\sqrt{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}a_{N}}.

To make a clear presentation, we impose a trivial ordering for elements in
\Lambda_{N}.
For any
I_{1}=\left(i_{1},j_{1}\right)\in\Lambda_{N}
and
I_{2}=\left(i_{2},j_{2}\right)\in

\Lambda_{N},
we say
I_{1}<I_{2}
if
i_{1}<i_{2}
or
i_{1}=i_{2}
but
j_{1}<j_{2}.

Recall that under certain assumptions,
Baltagi et al. 2016
established the asymptotic property of
S_{N}
that under the null hypothesis,

S_{N}/{\hat{\sigma}}_{S_{N}}\to\mathcal{N}(0,1)
in distribution when
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty.

Since the assumptions in this paper are slightly different from those in
Baltagi et al. 2016
,
we reconsider the asymptotic properties of
S_{N}
under our
Assumptions
1
-
3
. Similar to Theorems 2-3 in
Baltagi et al. 2016
, Lemma
S.1.10
presents the asymptotic null distribution of
S_{N}
,
and Lemma
S.1.11
presents that
\hat{\sigma}_{S_{N}}
is a ratio-consistent estimator of the variance of
S_{N}
.

Lemma S.1.10
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
and the null hypothesis,
we have
S_{N}/{\sigma}_{S_{N}}\to\mathcal{N}(0,1)
in distribution, as
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
with
\underset{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}N/T=\gamma\in(0,+\infty).

Here,

\mathbf{M}_{i}=\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{P}_{i},
for any
1\leq i\leq N,

and

\sigma^{2}_{S_{N}}=\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j}\right)/\{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{i}\right)\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{j}\right)\}.

Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{S_{N}}=\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\underset{1\leq i<j\leq N}{\sum\sum}v_{j}^{\prime}\left(v_{i}-\bar{v}_{ij}\right)v_{i}^{\prime}\left(v_{j}-\bar{v}_{ij}\right),

(S.1.15)

\bar{v}_{ij}=\sum_{1<k\neq i,j<N}v_{k}/(N-2)
and
v_{k}=\hat{\epsilon}_{k\cdot}/\|\hat{\epsilon}_{k\cdot}\|
for all
1\leq k\leq N.

Lemma S.1.11
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
and the null hypothesis, as
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
with
\underset{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}N/T=\gamma\in(0,+\infty)
, we have
\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{S_{N}}/{\sigma}^{2}_{S_{N}}\to 1
in probability.

Next, the following lemmas are provided for establishing the asymptotic independence between the two test statistics
S_{N}

and
L_{N}.

Lemma S.1.12
.

Assume that Assumptions
1
-
3
hold, under
H_{0},
if
\tilde{T}_{max}-4\log N+\log\log N
and
S_{N}/\sigma_{S_{N}}
are asymptotically independent,
then
L_{N}{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}})}/{\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}-4\log N+\log\log N
and
S_{N}/\hat{\sigma}_{S_{N}}
are also asymptotically
independent.

Lemma S.1.13
.

Under
H_{0}
and same assumptions in Lemma
S.1.12
, let

H(N,k)=\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{k}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right).

Then
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}H(N,k)=0.

Lemma S.1.14
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
S.1.12
,

\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{k}\in\Lambda_{N}}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right)\right]\rightarrow 0

as
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
for each
k\geq 1.

Next, we are ready to prove asymptotic independence stated in Theorem
2.4
.
By (
S.1.4
) and Lemma
S.1.10
, the following hold,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}-4\log N+\log\log N\rightarrow G(y)\,\text{ in distribution; }

(S.1.16)

\displaystyle\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\rightarrow N(0,1)\text{ in distribution, }

(S.1.17)

where
G(y)=\exp\left\{-\exp\left(-y/2\right)/\sqrt{8\pi}\right\}.

To show asymptotic independence, according to Lemma
S.1.12
, it is enough to show the limit of

\displaystyle\lim_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}\leq a_{N}\right)=\Phi(x)\cdot G(y),

(S.1.18)

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\Phi(x)=(2\pi)^{-1/2}\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{-t^{2}/2}dt
and

a_{N}=4\log N-\log\log N+y,

which makes sense for large
N
. Because of (
S.1.16
) and (
S.1.17
), the above is equivalent to that

\displaystyle\lim_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)=\Phi(x)\cdot\left\{1-G(y)\right\},

(S.1.19)

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Review notation
\Lambda_{N},A_{N}
and
B_{I}
for any
I=

(i,j)\in\Lambda_{N}
in (
S.1.4
). Write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}A_{N}B_{I}\right).

(S.1.20)

Here the notation
{A}_{N}B_{I}
stands for
{A}_{N}\cap B_{I}
. From the inclusion-exclusion principle,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}A_{N}B_{I}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots+

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l+1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l+1}}\right)

(S.1.21)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}A_{N}B_{I}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots-

(S.1.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l}}\right)

(S.1.23)

for any integer
l\geq 1
. Reviewing the definition

H(N,k)=\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{k}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right),

for
k\geq 1
in Lemma
\ref{linear},
we have from the lemma that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}H(N,k)=0.

(S.1.24)

Set

\zeta(p,k)=\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{k}\in\Lambda_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{k}}\right)\right\},

for
T\geq 1.
By Lemma
S.1.14
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\zeta(N,k)=0,

(S.1.25)

for each
k\geq 1
. The assertion (
S.1.4
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}A_{N}B_{I}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\left\{\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots-\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l}}\right)\right\}+\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{2l}\zeta(N,k)\right\}+H(N,2l+1)

\displaystyle\leq P

\displaystyle\left(A_{N}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}B_{I}\right)+\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{2l}\zeta(N,k)\right\}+H(N,2l+1),

(S.1.26)

where the inclusion-exclusion formula is used again in the last inequality, that is

\begin{array}[]{r}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}B_{I}\right)\geq\left\{\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots-\right.\\
\left.\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l}}\right)\right\},\end{array}

for all
l\geq 1
. By the definition of
a_{N}
and (
S.1.16
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}B_{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}-4\log N+\log\log N>y\right\}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 1-G(y),

as
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
. By (
S.1.17
),
\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\rightarrow\Phi(x)
as
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
. From (
S.1.20
), by fixing
l
first and sending
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
, we get from (
S.1.25
) that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Phi(x)\cdot\left\{1-G(y)\right\}+\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}H(N,2l+1).

Now, let
l\rightarrow\infty
and use (
S.1.24
) to see

\displaystyle\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)\leq\Phi(x)\cdot\left\{1-G(y)\right\}.

(S.1.27)

By applying the same argument to (
S.1.22
), we see that the counterpart of
(
S.1.4
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}A_{N}B_{I}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\left\{\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l-1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l-1}}\right)\right\}+

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{2l-1}\zeta(N,k)\right\}-H(N,2l)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{N}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}B_{I}\right)+\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{2l-1}\zeta(N,k)\right\}-H(N,2l),

where in the last step we use the inclusion-exclusion principle such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\in\Lambda_{N}}B_{I}\right)\leq\left\{\sum_{I_{1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}\right)-\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\right)+\cdots+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{I_{1}<I_{2}<\cdots<I_{2l-1}\in\Lambda_{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(B_{I_{1}}B_{I_{2}}\cdots B_{I_{2l-1}}\right)\right\},

for all
l\geq 1
. Review (
S.1.20
) and repeat the earlier procedure to see

\limsup_{\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{N}}{\sigma_{S_{N}}}\leq x,\max_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\frac{\left(\epsilon_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\right)^{2}}{\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}>a_{N}\right)\geq\Phi(x)\cdot\left\{1-G(y)\right\},

by sending
\min(N,T)\rightarrow\infty
and then sending
l\rightarrow\infty
. This and (
S.1.27
) yield (
S.1.19
). The proof is completed due to Lemma
S.1.12
.

\Box

S.2
Proofs of the lemmas

In this section, we will prove the lemmas used in the previous section.

S.2.1
Proof of Lemma
S.1.6

Recall that
\mathbf{M}_{i}=\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{P}_{i},

\mathbf{B}_{i}=\mathbf{x}_{i}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}.

(\mathbf{A})_{ij}
is the element at row
i
and column
j
of matrix

\mathbf{A},
for any square matrix
\mathbf{A}.
Hence, we define the element at row
i
and column
j
of matrix
\mathbf{M}_{l}=\mathbf{P}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{P}_{l}
as
\sigma_{ij,l},

1\leq i\leq j\leq N.
Let
\omega_{ij,l}=\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{l}e_{i}e_{j}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{l}e_{i}e_{j}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}\leq\sigma_{ii,l}\sigma_{jj,l},

for
1\leq i<j\leq T;1\leq l\leq N.

Then, we claim that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}),\,\,\,\,\Big|\Big(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{l}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}),\,\,\,\,\,\Big|\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{s}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}),

(S.2.1)

for any
1\leq l,s\leq N,\,1\leq i,j\leq T.

To simplify the expression, we let
b_{ij,l}\doteq\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\Big)_{ij},

for any
1\leq l\leq N,\,1\leq i,j\leq T.
Under Assumption
2
, we have
\max_{1\leq j\leq N}|b_{ij,l}|=O(T^{-1}).

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{T}b_{ik,l}\sigma_{kj}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{T}|b_{ik,l}||\sigma_{kj}|

\displaystyle\leq O(T^{-1})\sum_{k=1}^{T}|\sigma_{kj}|.

Due to Assumption
3
, we have
\sum_{k=1}^{T}\left|\sigma_{jk}\right|^{\tau}\leq C
for some
0<\tau<2
and
1\leq j\leq T.
So, if
0<\tau<1,
we have
\sum_{k=1}^{T}|\sigma_{kj}|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{T}|\sigma_{kj}|^{\tau}|\sigma_{kj}|^{1-\tau}=O(1).

If
1<\tau<2,
from a convex inequality we have

\left\{\big(\sum_{k=1}^{T}|\sigma_{kj}|\big)^{\tau}\right\}^{1/\tau}\leq\left\{T^{\tau-1}\sum_{k=1}^{T}|\sigma_{kj}|^{\tau}\right\}^{1/\tau}\leq O(T^{1-\frac{1}{\tau}}).

So,
\Big|\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}).

Similarly, we have
\Big|\Big(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{l}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}})
and
\Big|\Big(\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{s}\Big)_{ij}\Big|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}).

Then, due to
\mathbf{P}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{P}_{l}=\mathbf{\Sigma}-\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{l}+\mathbf{B}_{l}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{l},

we can conclude that

\left|\sum_{l=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma_{ij,l}-\sigma_{ij}}{N}\right|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}})

and

\left|\sum_{l=1}^{N}\frac{\sigma_{ii,l}\sigma_{jj,l}+\sigma_{ij,l}^{2}-\sigma_{ii}\sigma_{jj}-\sigma^{2}_{ij}}{N}\right|=O(T^{-1\vee-\frac{1}{\tau}}).

Recall that
\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}=\sum_{l=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}/N,

1\leq j\leq N,

we have
\hat{\sigma}_{ij}=\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{li}\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}-\frac{N}{N-1}\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}
Since
\mathrm{cov}\left(\hat{\epsilon}_{li},\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}\right)=\sigma_{ij,l}
, we obtain that
\mathrm{var}\left(\hat{\epsilon}_{li}\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}\right)=\sigma_{ij,l}^{2}+\sigma_{ii,l}\sigma_{jj,l}+\gamma_{2}\omega_{ij,l}.

By classical Cramér type large deviation results for independent random variables (see Corollary 3.1 in
Saulis and Statulevicius 1991
), we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\epsilon}_{li}\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}-\sigma_{ij,l}\right)}{\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{N}\left(\sigma_{ij,l}^{2}+(1+|\gamma_{2}|)\sigma_{ii,l}\sigma_{jj,l}\right)}}\Big|\geq x\Big)

(S.2.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\epsilon}_{li}\hat{\epsilon}_{lj}-\sigma_{ij,l}\right)}{\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{N}\left(\sigma_{ii,l}\sigma_{jj,l}+\sigma_{ij,l}^{2}+\gamma_{2}\omega_{ij,l}\right)}}\Big|\geq x\Big)

(S.2.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}(1-\varepsilon)\Big\}

(S.2.4)

uniformly in
x\in\big[0,o(\sqrt{N})\big).
For
\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}
, we have
\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right)=\sum_{l=1}^{N}\sigma_{jj,l}/N^{2}.
By (
S.2.1
),
\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right)\leq CN^{-1},
uniformly in
1\leq j\leq T.
Again, by classical Cramér type large deviation results for independent random variables, we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right|\geq x\sqrt{\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right)}\right)\leq C\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}(1-\varepsilon)\Big\}

uniformly in
x\in\big[0,o(\sqrt{N})\big).
So

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right|\geq x^{2}\sqrt{\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\right)\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right)}\right)\leq 2C\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}(1-\varepsilon)\Big\}

uniformly in
x\in\big[0,o(\sqrt{N})\big).
We have, uniformly for
x\in\big[0,o(\sqrt{N})\big),x^{2}\sqrt{\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\right)\mathrm{var}\left(\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\right)}=o(x/\sqrt{N})
. So for any
\delta>0
and large
N

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot i}\bar{\hat{\epsilon}}_{\cdot j}\right|\geq\delta\frac{x}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\leq 2C\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}(1-\varepsilon)\Big\}

(S.2.5)

uniformly for
x\in\big[0,o(\sqrt{N})\big).
Hence,
the lemma follows from (
S.2.2
) and (
S.2.5
).

\Box

S.2.2
Proof of Lemma
S.1.7

So, according to the definitions in Lemma
S.1.7
,
we have

\displaystyle\|\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2},

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{tr}(\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}})\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle T^{2}a_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{N}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle T^{2}b_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\left[\sum_{i=1}\sum_{j=1}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\left(\sum_{i=1}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}-\sum_{i=1}\sum_{j=1}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}+\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}\right].

It is easy to verify that
a_{N}
and
b_{N}
will make the equation (
S.1.10
) true. In the following, we will prove that
a_{N},b_{N}
satisfy the properties in the lemma.

We first deal with the term
a_{N}.
Recall that
\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}
and
\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{P}_{i}=\mathbf{M}_{i},
we have

\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j}),&i\neq j,\\
&2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\},&i=j.\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore,

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}=\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}.

Moreover, by Lemma
S.1.4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot})+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{j}).

So we have

\displaystyle{T^{2}}\gamma_{N}a_{N}

\displaystyle=\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{j})\Big]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})\left(1-\frac{1}{T}\right)+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\left\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})-\frac{1}{T}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{j})\right\}

\displaystyle-(1-\frac{1}{T})|\gamma_{2}|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{T}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma})\right\}+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\left\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{j})\right.

\displaystyle-\left.2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{i})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i})+2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{j})\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\left\{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma})\right\}-|\gamma_{2}|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma})\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle N\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})-2pN\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma})C-\frac{N\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T}+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\left\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})-\frac{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})}{T}\right\}

\displaystyle-10pC^{2}N(N-1)-\frac{p^{2}C^{2}N(N-1)}{T}-|\gamma_{2}|C^{2}NT-|\gamma_{2}|NpC^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle T^{2}\gamma_{N}\left(1-\frac{c_{1}}{T}\right),

for some constant
c_{1}>0.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{T^{2}}\gamma_{N}a_{N}

\displaystyle=\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{j})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\left\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})+2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j})+2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbf{B}_{j})+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j})+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j})-2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{j}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i})\right\}+2C^{2}NT

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})+\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i})\right\}+(1-\frac{1}{T})|\gamma_{2}|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{2})+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma})\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{2}N(N-1)T+11pC^{2}N(N-1)+2C^{2}NT+N\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma})+p^{2}C^{2}N

\displaystyle+|\gamma_{2}|C^{2}NT+|\gamma_{2}|NpC^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T^{2}\gamma_{N}\left(1+\frac{c_{2}N}{T}\right),

for some constant
c_{2}>0.
This proves that
a_{N}
satisfies the inequality in the lemma.

It remains to calculate
b_{N}.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{var}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}\bigg\}^{2}\Bigg]-\mathbb{E}^{2}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}\bigg\}.

Note that
i\neq j\neq k
means that
i,j,k
are not equal, and

i\neq j\neq k\neq l
means that
i,j,k,l
are not equal.
First, by Lemma
S.1.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}\bigg\}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot})^{4}\right\}+2\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{4}\right\}+\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot})^{2}(\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+4\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot})^{2}\right\}+4\underset{i\neq j\neq k}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{k\cdot})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+2\underset{i\neq j\neq k}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot})^{2}(\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{k\cdot})^{2}\right\}+\underset{i\neq j\neq k\neq l}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\hat{\epsilon}_{i\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{j\cdot})^{2}(\hat{\epsilon}_{k\cdot}^{\prime}\hat{\epsilon}_{l\cdot})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big\{48\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{4}_{i})+32\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{3}_{i})\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+12\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})+12\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}^{2}_{i})\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}^{4}(\mathbf{M}_{i})

\displaystyle+\gamma_{2}f_{\gamma_{2}}+\gamma_{4}f_{\gamma_{4}}+\gamma_{6}f_{\gamma_{6}}+\gamma_{1}^{2}f_{\gamma_{1}^{2}}+\gamma_{2}^{2}f_{\gamma_{2}^{2}}+\gamma_{1}\gamma_{3}f_{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{3}}\Big\}

\displaystyle+2\underset{i\neq j}{\sum^{N}\sum^{N}}\Big[6\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})^{2}+3\mathrm{tr}^{2}(\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{M}_{j})+3\gamma_{2}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{M}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}

\displaystyle+\gamma_{2}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}\{(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{j}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\circ(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{P}_{j}\epsilon_{j\cdot}\epsilon_{j\cdot}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}_{j}\mathbf{P}_{i}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\}\Big]

\displaystyle=
